(a) The energies of the particle block against the momentum for the two isospin components: the formula (curves) and the eigenvalues of the lattice operator (circles); the dotted lines mark the momenta $q^1_{\rm LL}=\mathsf A^\downarrow_{{\rm LL},1}$ and $q^1_{\rm LL}=\mathsf A^\uparrow_{{\rm LL},1}$ at which the two components have their lowest energies. (b) The probability $P^\uparrow_{\rm LL}$ of isospin up against the time for $A^1_{{\rm LL},0}=1.5$: the exact solution (curve) and the integrations by IDA and by CVODE (points, in turn).

![](figures/notebook_figure4ab.png){width=58%}

**Figure 4 of the notebook, panels (a) and (b) (checks AS-3 and AS-4).** Case B, the uniform polar superfluid. (a) The positive energies $E^-_{\rm LL}$ and $E^+_{\rm LL}$ on the lattice against the momentum: the formulas (curves), the eigenvalues of the lattice operator (circles) and the energy of the normal state (dashed); the gap $\Delta_{\rm LL}$ opens at the Fermi momentum. (b) The probability $P^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}$ of the hole against the time: the exact solution (curve) and the two integrations (points).

![](figures/notebook_figure4c.png){width=58%}

**Figure 4 of the notebook, panel (c) (check AS-5).** The gap $\Delta_{\rm LL}$ of massless spinor charges against the coupling $G_{\rm LL}/G^{\rm crit}_{\rm LL}$ for three chemical potentials, from the gap equation in closed form (curves); the squares are the gaps that were inserted into the integral computed by quadrature.

![](figures/notebook_figure5ab.png){width=58%}

**Figure 5 of the notebook, panels (a) and (b) (check AS-9).** Case C, a domain wall of the pair field on a ring of the length 24. (a) The pair field with a kink and an antikink. (b) The density of the bound states: the analytic density of the two walls (curve) and the summed density of the eight lattice eigenvectors near $+m_{\rm LL}$ (circles).

![](figures/notebook_figure5cd.png){width=58%}

**Figure 5 of the notebook, panels (c) and (d) (check AS-9).** (c) The eigenvalues of the ring near zero against their number; the 16 bound states (orange) lie inside the gap, near $\pm m_{\rm LL}$. (d) The convergence of the analytic bound state with the lattice spacing, on two logarithmic axes: the shift $E^{\rm R}_{\rm LL}-m_{\rm LL}$ of its energy, the first-order shift $r^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}/6$ (dashed), and the relative residual.

![](figures/notebook_figure6.png){width=70%}

**Figure 6 of the notebook (check AS-10).** Case D, the Landau levels of the two isospin components against the number $n^{\rm L}_{\rm LL}$ of the level: the formula (curves) and the eigenvalues of the diagonalization in the oscillator basis (circles). Every level from 1 on is a pair of equal eigenvalues; the level 0 occurs once.

# Appendix A. How the notebook was built and evaluated

**The source.** The notebook is written as a text file in the Wolfram package format, `notebook_source/3+1super-loop.source.wl`: every cell is introduced by a marker line such as `(* ::Section:: *)`, `(* ::Text:: *)` or `(* ::Input:: *)`. Inside an input cell a symbol is written as `LL["tex"]` (for example `LL["G_{\\rm LL}"]`), and a line `(* @@INCLUDE file@@ *)` inserts a file: the packages `sl31_core.wl` (the reader of symbols and the symbolization) and `sl31_formula.wl` (the reader of formulas), the registry `registry_data.wl`, and the list `all_symbols.wl` of all 440 symbols for test S-14 (written by `tools/gen_all_symbols.wls`). A text cell holds its text with formulas in TeX between dollar signs, code between backquotes and bold text between double asterisks; a cell `(* ::DisplayFormula:: *)` holds the TeX of one formula that stands on lines of its own.

**The source of Part II** is kept in separate files, one or two per chapter (`notebook_source/part2_A_core.source.wl` for chapter II.1, `part2_B_item1.source.wl` and `part2_C_item1b.source.wl` for chapter II.2, `part2_D_item2.source.wl` for chapter II.3); a line `(* @@INCLUDECELLS file@@ *)` at the end of the main source inserts such a file with its own cell markers. A line `(* @@INCLUDE t2_fields_figure_data.wl@@ *)` inserts the numbers of the data files of item [1] (made by `tools/make_fig_data_cell.py`), a line `(* @@INCLUDE t2_analytic_data.wl@@ *)` those of item [2] (made by `tools/make_item2_data_cell.py`), and the line `(* @@COMPONENTS@@ *)` of the first cell of Part II is replaced by the builder with the list of all components of families typed in the notebook (section 10.4). A cell that begins with the marker `(* ::Input::Closed:: *)` becomes a closed input cell (option `CellOpen -> False`): the notebook evaluates it like every other cell, but shows only its cell bracket, and the printed form leaves it out. The two cells with the numbers of the data files are such cells (decision of Appendix C; before the build of the notebook this was tested on a small notebook with one closed cell, which the front end evaluated and did not print).

**The builder** `tools/build_notebook.wls` (run by wolframscript) turns the source into the notebook `notebook_source/3+1super-loop.unevaluated.nb`: the code of every input cell is parsed into boxes by the front end itself (so comments, line breaks and indentation are kept), every `LL["tex"]` is replaced by the two-dimensional box of the symbol, and every text cell receives its inline formulas, its code font and its bold text from the reader of formulas of the notebook itself (`SL31TextData`, section 6.2; until 18 November 2026 from the TeX importer, errors E17 and E22). The builder loads the two packages for this, counts the formulas, and turns every problem that the reader reports for a formula into a warning. The notebook is written by the kernel, not through the front end (error E9). The build of the final notebook reported 17 components of families, 294 cells, 2 closed input cells, 917 formulas in text cells and 0 warnings in 4.6 seconds (`logs/build_notebook.txt`).

**Dry runs.** A build, the copy of the notebook, its evaluation in the front end and the export of the pages form a long cycle, and an error of the code should be found before it. Before a build, the code is therefore evaluated once by wolframscript, without the notebook: `tools/make_dryrun.py` writes the code of the input cells of Part II into one file (all but the first cell, which holds the check T2-SYM and needs Part I and the front end). It takes the input cell of Part I that defines `SL31Check` from the main source, so that the numbers of the details are written as text by the functions of the notebook itself, and replaces only the printing by one text line with the name, the details and the computing time; every reference `LL["tex"]` is replaced by its TeX (in Part II the symbols are only labels); `tools/make_dryrun_part1.py` writes the cells of Part I that concern the reader of formulas (the reader itself, the registry, the examples and test S-15; the package `sl31_core.wl` is loaded before them). The dry runs of the final sources gave 31 of 31 checks of Part II in 57 seconds (`logs/dryrun_part2.txt`) and the passed test S-15 (`logs/dryrun_part1.txt`). A file given after the option `--append` is copied to the end of the dry run unchanged; the measurements of the local errors of the integrations of item [2] (`tools/item2_ida_local_errors.wl`, section 13.4) were made in this way, because they need the objects of the chapters before them (`logs/IDA_item2_local_errors.txt`). The dry runs are a help for the work only: the evaluation of the notebook in the front end stays the evidence for every result of this document.

**The evaluation** is done by the front end of Wolfram.app, exactly as Evaluation > Evaluate Notebook does it (file `tools/guirun.wl`), in a kernel named `SL31Run` that is declared for the running session only (`SetOptions[$FrontEndSession, EvaluatorNames -> ...]`, so the preferences of Wolfram.app are not changed). It takes three calls, each of which returns at once. (1) `start`: the notebook is opened (visible) and its evaluator is set to `SL31Run`; the kernel is started by the evaluation of a hidden one-cell notebook in it, and nothing else is queued. That cell sets the variable `$Post` of the kernel, so that the kernel appends, after every input line, the number of the line and the time to a progress file (the function given to `$Post` returns every result unchanged); it writes a small file that says that the kernel runs and answers; and it sets the line counter `$Line` to 0, so that the first cell of the notebook is `In[1]`. (2) `go`, when that file exists: `FrontEndTokenExecute[nb, "EvaluateNotebook"]` starts the evaluation of all cells, and a second hidden one-cell notebook, queued after it in the same kernel, writes the test list of the kernel to a results file and then quits the kernel, so that every run starts with a fresh kernel. (3) `status`: while the results file does not exist it reports the last entry of the progress file, without any call to the front end; when it exists, the evaluated notebook is saved (with its evaluator reset to the default) and exported to PDF, and a log lists every message and every printed line with the label of its input cell (`logs/3+1super-loop.evaluation.log.txt`). Until 18 November 2026 one call started the kernel and queued the notebook at once, and nothing recorded the progress; the run of that day at 12:19 Pacific time stopped in that state (error E37), and the three calls and the progress file are the fix. The progress file of the final run is kept (`logs/3+1super-loop.evaluation.progress.txt`): the kernel answered at 13:17:56, the input line 1 was finished at 13:18:04 and the last one, line 886, at 13:18:41. The commands of `guirun.wl` are sent to the kernel of the running Wolfram.app session through a small file-request mechanism (`tools/watcher.wl`, `tools/handler.wl`): a scheduled task looks every 3 seconds for request files in a scratch folder.

**To reproduce** without any of these tools: open `notebook_source/3+1super-loop.unevaluated.nb` (or the evaluated `3+1super-loop.nb`) in Wolfram and choose Evaluation > Evaluate Notebook; the result must be the same 48 PASS lines and no message. The commands below are run in the folder `3+1super-loop_provenance`. To rebuild the unevaluated notebook from the source:

```
wolframscript -file tools/build_notebook.wls notebook_source/3+1super-loop.source.wl \
    notebook_source/3+1super-loop.unevaluated.nb notebook_source/sl31_core.wl \
    notebook_source/sl31_formula.wl
```

(the source includes its files relative to its own folder; `registry_data.wl` is made by `tools/registry_to_wl.wls` from the CSV file of the registry, and `all_symbols.wl` by `tools/gen_all_symbols.wls`; the absolute paths inside these two scripts name the scratch folder of the author's Mac and must be adapted elsewhere).

(the files of Part II and the two files of data stand in the same folder as the source). The two data cells are remade from the data files of the original programs with

```
python3 tools/make_fig_data_cell.py ../Revision_SuperLoop/test2/data \
    notebook_source/t2_fields_figure_data.wl
python3 tools/make_item2_data_cell.py ../Revision_SuperLoop/test2/data \
    notebook_source/t2_analytic_data.wl
```

The comparison of the reader of formulas with the TeX importer (section 6.2) is repeated with

```
python3 tools/formula_inventory.py notebook_source notebook_source/formula_inventory.wl
wolframscript -file tools/formula_compare.wls notebook_source/sl31_core.wl \
    notebook_source/sl31_formula.wl notebook_source/formula_inventory.wl report.txt
```

and the dry runs, in a scratch folder, with

```
mkdir -p /tmp/dryrun && cp notebook_source/sl31_core.wl /tmp/dryrun/
python3 tools/make_dryrun.py /tmp/dryrun/dryrun_part2.wl notebook_source/part2_A_core.source.wl \
    notebook_source/part2_B_item1.source.wl notebook_source/part2_C_item1b.source.wl \
    notebook_source/part2_D_item2.source.wl --append tools/item2_ida_local_errors.wl
python3 tools/make_dryrun_part1.py /tmp/dryrun/dryrun_part1.wl notebook_source
wolframscript -file /tmp/dryrun/dryrun_part2.wl
wolframscript -file /tmp/dryrun/dryrun_part1.wl
```

(the file of the second dry run loads `sl31_core.wl` from the folder in which it stands, hence the copy).

**Mechanical checks of the sources and of the pages.** Four scripts check rules that a reader cannot check by eye over hundreds of pages; each prints PASS lines or the list of what breaks its rule, and each is run before a build or after an export (all four in Python 3; the outputs of the final state are in `logs`).

- `tools/check_notebook_symbols.py` applies the three rules of the symbols of the project to every formula of the text cells of the notebook: the letters $\mu$, $\Lambda$, $\Omega$, $\nu$, $\Delta$, $\zeta$ and $\varepsilon$ always carry the suffix LL; no Latin letter stands without the suffix except the index letters $j$ and $p$, the dimension count $D$, the imaginary unit and the differential d, and the Greek letters that are symbols of the project carry the suffix as well (except the index $\iota$ and the number $\pi$); every letter in a bold, sans-serif, calligraphic, double-struck or Gothic alphabet is a registered symbol. It uses the functions of the script that checks the documents of the folder `Revision_SuperLoop` (`Revision_SuperLoop/code/scripts/check_symbols.py`), so that the notebook and those documents obey the same rules. It also checks that every reference `LL["tex"]` of an input cell names exactly one item of the registry. A formula that shows a symbol without its suffix on purpose is listed in the script with its reason. Result for the final sources: 917 formulas, 0 break a rule, 2 are listed exceptions (the letters $\Delta$ and $m$ of section I.1 of the notebook, which are named there as examples of letters with several meanings in the literature); 110 references, all of them items of the registry (`logs/check_notebook_symbols.txt`). The same script found 12 formulas that broke a rule in the notebook as it was committed before (error E30).
- `tools/check_notebook_names.py` checks "one name, one object" for the names of the program. A notebook is evaluated in one session: a name that receives a value at the top level of a cell keeps it in all later cells. The script reads the input cells chapter by chapter and requires that a name that is global in an earlier chapter is used in a later chapter only if a table of the script declares it as the same object (for example the couplings and the colors of the figures), and that no global name is used as a local name (a variable of `Module`, `With` or `Block`, the variable of a loop, the argument of a function). Result: 4 chapters, 682 global names, 0 errors (`logs/check_notebook_names.txt`); 12 errors in the notebook as it was committed before (error E33).
- `tools/check_pdf_right_edge.py` reads the exported pages as pictures (150 dots per inch) and reports every page on which at least three rows of the picture hold ink beyond the right edge of the text area, 553.5 points from the left edge of the page of 612 points (the running head is left out; a hanging hyphen, one or two rows, is listed but allowed). The front end cuts what passes this edge, so that such a page has lost a part of a line or of a figure. Result: 0 of the 253 pages (`logs/check_pdf_right_edge.txt`); 4 pages of the first exported pages of chapter II.3 (errors E31 and E32, `logs/E31_right_edge_first_export.txt`).
- `tools/check_provenance_symbols.py` checks this document: every symbol with the suffix LL that a formula outside section 2 uses must be defined in section 2. Two symbols are the same for this check if they differ only in the values of their indices. The script that builds the `.tex` and `.pdf` versions runs it and stops if a symbol is not defined. Result: section 2 defines 154 symbols, the other sections use 149, and every one of them is defined; one use is a listed exception, a wrongly printed label that the description of error E18 quotes on purpose (`logs/check_provenance_symbols.txt`). In the document as it was committed before, 3 symbols were not defined (error E39).

The script `tools/chapter_texts_to_md.py` writes the texts of one chapter of the notebook as Markdown, without a change of a word or of a formula; section 13.2 of this document is its output for chapter II.3 (the commands are given at the end of this appendix).

**The pictures of the evidence.** The pictures in `logs` that show pages before and after a fix (errors E22 to E25, E28, E31 and E32), the picture of Figure 2 in section 12.3 and the pictures of the Figures 3 to 6 in section 13.5 are made by `tools/make_evidence_pictures.py` from the exported pages. The script turns a page into a picture with the program pdftoppm and reads the positions of the words of the page with the program pdftotext (both belong to the Poppler tools); a piece of a page is named in the script by words of the page, and its edges are moved to the nearest empty row of the picture (or, in the table of the symbols, to the nearest horizontal rule), so that no line is cut. The pages before the fixes of the errors E22 to E28 come from the exported file of the notebook as it was committed before (commit 213d205 of the repository, 181 pages); Figure 2 before its fix, the end of the line of check AS-10 before its fix and Figure 3 before its fix are kept as the pictures `logs/E25_figure2_before_fix.png`, `logs/E31_check_line_before_fix.png` and `logs/E32_figure3_before_fix.png`, because the exported pages of those evaluations were replaced by the next ones. The pages of the present notebook are found by words that they hold, not by their numbers, which every new chapter changes. The pictures are remade with

```
git show 213d205:3+1super-loop_provenance/export/3+1super-loop.pdf > old.pdf
python3 tools/make_evidence_pictures.py --old-pdf old.pdf --new-pdf export/3+1super-loop.pdf \
    --render-test logs/E22_formula_render_test.pdf --fig2-before logs/E25_figure2_before_fix.png \
    --spacing-test logs/E28_letter_digit_spacing_test.pdf --out logs \
    --figure2 figures/notebook_figure2_gs13.png \
    --line-before logs/E31_check_line_before_fix.png --fig3-before logs/E32_figure3_before_fix.png \
    --figures-dir figures
```

(Python 3 with the packages Pillow and numpy, and the Poppler tools). The mechanical checks and the texts of section 13.2 are remade, in the top folder of the repository, with

```
python3 3+1super-loop_provenance/tools/check_notebook_symbols.py
python3 3+1super-loop_provenance/tools/check_notebook_names.py
python3 3+1super-loop_provenance/tools/check_pdf_right_edge.py \
    3+1super-loop_provenance/export/3+1super-loop.pdf
python3 3+1super-loop_provenance/tools/check_provenance_symbols.py \
    3+1super-loop_provenance/P_3+1super-loop.md
python3 3+1super-loop_provenance/tools/chapter_texts_to_md.py \
    3+1super-loop_provenance/notebook_source/part2_D_item2.source.wl --level 3 --number 13.2 \\
    --split-wide 300
```

**Copies to the author's computer.** The files are written in a workspace and copied into the scratch folder of the author's Mac before each evaluation. A copy can deliver an earlier version of a file whose name was used for a copy shortly before; this happened twice during the first stage of Part II (a test script and the builder arrived in their previous versions) and once more on 18 November 2026, during the experiments with IDA (error E27). Since then every file is copied from a staging file whose name begins with the first eight digits of the MD5 digest of its content, so that a name is never used for two contents, and the digest of every copied file is compared on both sides before a build or an evaluation; for the final evaluation the digests of all sources, of the two packages and of the builder agreed.

# Appendix B. Errors found, their causes and their fixes

Every error below was met while the notebook was prepared and evaluated. E1 to E14 belong to Part I, E15 to E19 to the first stage of Part II, E20 to E29 to the change of 18 November 2026 (the time integration by IDA, and the reader of formulas; E29 is an error of this document itself), and E30 to E40 to the stage of item [2] of the same day (E39 and E40 are errors of this document; E36 lists errors of the original program that the rewriting found; E38 is open); E41 is an error of an experiment made for E38. With the one exception of E38, each is fixed in the final notebook.

**E1. The symbolized box was never recognized when the notebook was evaluated by a program.** Cause: the Notation package takes the default of the option `WorkingForm` from `AbsoluteCurrentValue[InputNotebook[], {CommonDefaultFormatTypes, "Output"}]`; without a selected input notebook this is `$Failed`, and the reading rule was stored for the form `$Failed` (found by reading the source code of the package, `Notation.m`, in the installation). Fix: `WorkingForm -> StandardForm` is given explicitly in every call of `Symbolize`. Evidence: the tests S-2 and S-14.

**E2. Results in plain text.** `NotebookEvaluate[nb, InsertResults -> True]`, run by wolframscript, inserted the results as plain-text output form instead of typeset boxes. A first remedy was a custom cell-by-cell evaluator (`tools/run_notebook.wls`); it was replaced by the evaluation in the front end of Wolfram.app (E6).

**E3. Code shown in linear syntax.** Input cells written as plain strings showed the symbols as linear syntax (for example `\!\(\*SubscriptBox[...]\)`). Fix: the builder parses the code into boxes with the front end and inserts the boxes of the symbols.

**E4. The TeX importer loses alphabets.** `ImportString["$...$", "TeX"]` turns `\mathbb`, `\mathsf`, `\mathfrak` and `\mathbf` letters into plain letters, so different symbols would coincide. Fix: the notebook's own converter (section 6.1); the TeX importer was then still used for the formulas in text (it is not used at all since error E22).

**E5. Equal names for styled letters.** The Notation package builds names after removing `StyleBox` and `TagBox`, so $\mathbf e_0$, $\mathsf e_0$ and $e_0$ would become one symbol. Fix: `SymbolizeRootName` with names that record the alphabet (section 8). Evidence: S-3.

**E6. The evaluator run by wolframscript failed.** Two full runs stopped without a result; a diagnostic run showed that a cell with several statements on separate lines was read by `ToExpression` as ONE product of the statements (the output of a 52-statement cell was `Null^52`), and that cells stored as lists of boxes returned unevaluated `HoldComplete[...]` results. In the front end the statements of a cell are separated at its line breaks before they are sent to the kernel. Fix: the evaluation was moved into the front end of Wolfram.app (Appendix A), which also answered the author's request to execute the notebook in his Wolfram.app; `tools/run_notebook.wls` is kept, marked as superseded.

**E7. Nine symbols were not recognized in typed input.** A test notebook in the front end showed that `Symbolize[ParsedBoxWrapper[SubscriptBox["a", "LL,p"]]]` is not matched by the typed $a_{{\rm LL},p}$: the sum $\sum_{{\rm LL}=1}^2{\rm LL}\,a_{{\rm LL},p}$ gave $a_{1,p}+2\,a_{2,p}$ and `AtomQ` gave `False`, and the saved input cell held `SubscriptBox["a", RowBox[{"LL", ",", "p"}]]` (folder `experiments`). To find every affected symbol, a notebook with one cell for each of the 430 boxes then symbolized was evaluated in the front end with `$PreRead` recording the boxes that the kernel received (`tools/canon_build.wls`, `tools/canon_compare.wls`): 9 differed, all strings of roman text with several tokens (`"int,F"`, `"int,B"`, `"ext,F"`, `"ext,B"`, `"v,Y"`, `"v,4"`, `"s(2)"`, `"s(3)"`, `"s(4)"`), which the front end had cut into `RowBox`es (`logs/E07_front_end_boxes_before_fix.txt`). The front end's own parser confirmed exactly these 9 among the 213 strings then present (`tools/leaf_check.wls`, `logs/E07_leaf_check_front_end_parser.txt`). Cause: before evaluation the front end reads every string of the boxes again as typed text (the reference page of `StripBoxes` states that the front end strips boxes "using a more extensive process" when it sends them to the kernel). Fix: the converter cuts roman text into tokens as the front end does (section 6); after the fix the same comparison found 0 differences (`logs/E07_front_end_boxes_after_fix.txt`). The test S-0 repeats the check in every evaluation.

**E8. Three operators were symbolized as letters.** The tensor product $\otimes$, the direct sum $\oplus$ and the composition $\circ$ are single characters and were classified as letters before the test for operators was reached. In an input cell the front end read them as operators and regrouped the whole line around them. Fix: the test for operators comes first; these three are operators and correspond to Wolfram functions (S-13). The number of symbolized TeX items fell from 430 to 427.

**E9. The cell of test S-14 was not evaluated.** In the first full evaluation the input cell that lists all symbols received an input label but no evaluation: no result, no message, and the next cell received the same input number. The stored cell showed a regrouped line: the partial-derivative sign with a subscript, $\partial_p$, had been read as an operator applied to everything after it (a `RowBox` that begins with a comma followed it). An experiment that split the list into cells of ten symbols (`tools/s14_build.wls`, `logs/E09_s14_chunk_experiment.log.txt`) located the problem in the part with $\otimes$, $\oplus$, $\circ$ and $\partial_p$. Evaluated alone in cells of the form `{b, 1}`, every symbol box reached the kernel unchanged (`tools/canon2_build.wls`, `tools/canon2_compare.wls`, `logs/E09_operand_check_in_kernel_input.txt`), and writing and reading the cells through the front end with `NotebookPut` and `NotebookGet` changed none of them either (`tools/reparse_check.wls`, `logs/E09_reparse_check.txt`); the regrouping had been made when the builder wrote the notebook file through the front end (`Export[file, notebook, "NB"]` inside `UsingFrontEnd`). Fix (three changes, made together, so that the evaluation does not separate their effects): (a) the builder writes the notebook expression with the kernel itself; (b) every symbol whose base is an operator sign, and every group name, ket and expression, is wrapped in `TagBox[box, "SL31Name", SyntaxForm -> "symbol"]`, which tells the front end to treat the box as one operand wherever it stands (the reference page of `SyntaxForm` describes the option; the value `"symbol"` for a box that should act as a standalone thing was recommended by the front-end developer J. Fultz on the MathGroup mailing list in January 2014); (c) a script on a closing parenthesis is attached to the whole group, the form that the front end makes of typed input. After the three changes the built notebook contains no regrouped line, and the test S-14 passes for all symbols (422 at that time, 440 now).

**E10. `Clear::ssym: 42 is not a symbol or a valid string pattern.`** The demonstration of section I.2 cleared the value of $a_{\rm LL}$ with `ReleaseHold[Hold[Clear] @@ s]`; `Hold[Clear][a_LL]` evaluates its argument before `ReleaseHold` acts, so `Clear` received 42. Fix: `Clear @@ s` and `Set @@ Join[s, HoldComplete[42]]` (section 5.4).

**E11. Warnings `Symbolize::bsymbexs`** ("the box structure attempting to be symbolized has a similar or identical symbol already defined"). Cause: TeX items with the same box (the four groups of section 7) were symbolized once each. Fix: the items are gathered by their boxes (`GatherBy`), and each box is symbolized once. The test S-3 was rewritten to compare boxes and symbols (it had compared TeX items with symbols and reported 430 against 425 as a failure).

**E12. TeX of the symbols showed the internal names.** A rule `Format[s, TeXForm] = ...` had no effect, because `TeXForm` converts the `TraditionalForm` boxes of an expression, and the Notation package defines only the `StandardForm` boxes. Fix: `MakeBoxes[s, TraditionalForm] = box` for every symbol. Evidence: S-5 (422 of 422 at that time, 440 of 440 now).

**E13. Plain-text names "Missing[NotAvailable]".** The CSV import of Wolfram 15 gives `Missing["NotAvailable"]` for an empty field; `ToString` made the name "Missing[NotAvailable]" for 14 entries, and the test S-12 reported it as a name shared by 14 entries. Fix: `tools/registry_to_wl.wls` turns every field into a string and an empty field into "" (no name).

**E14. Code spans with a context mark.** In text cells the builder marks code with backquotes; code that itself contains the context mark `` ` `` (for example `` SL31`Test`$Results ``) broke the code span. Fix: such code is marked with double backquotes.

**E15. GS-15 failed in the first evaluation of Part II (32 of 33 checks passed).** The connection was written `conn15[x_, p_] := -(Sp - Sm) (0.5/h15) . Sinv`, where `Sp` and `Sm` stand here for the spinor matrices at the two shifted points and `Sinv` for the inverse spinor matrix at the point. In the grammar of the Wolfram Language the matrix product `.` (`Dot`) binds more strongly than the product written by placing two expressions side by side (`Times`): in the table of the operators in order of decreasing precedence (tutorial "Operator Input Forms") `.` stands above the implicit product. The expression was therefore read as `-(Sp - Sm) * ((0.5/h15) . Sinv)`. `Dot` of a number and a matrix is not defined; it stays unevaluated without a message, so the "connection" was a matrix whose entries contained the unevaluated product, and the comparisons of the check could not give `True`. The evaluation gave no message, so only the check itself revealed the error. Evidence: `tools/e15_dot_precedence.wls` with its output `logs/E15_dot_precedence.txt`: the parsed form of `-(a - b) (s) . c` is `Times[Times[-1, Plus[a, Times[-1, b]]], Dot[s, c]]`; `0.5 . {{1., 2.}, {3., 4.}}` stays unevaluated (head `Dot`); the faulty form applied to numbers gives a matrix of unevaluated products; the corrected form `(-(0.5) (A - B)) . m` gives the expected matrix. Fix: the number multiplies the difference first, inside parentheses, `conn15[x_, p_] := (-(0.5/h15) (Sp - Sm)) . Sinv`, with a comment in the code. After the fix GS-15 passes with the deviations of the original program (section 12.2).

**E16. Labels of the figures covered curves; a label and the summary table were cut at the edge of the page.** Found by looking at the exported PDF (`logs/E16_figures_and_table_before_fix.png`): in Figure 1(a) a label crossed the vertical axis line at $q^1_{\rm LL}=0$, the label of the doubler for $r^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}=0$ lay on the orange curve, and the label $m_{\rm LL}=0.4$ touched the same curve; in Figure 1(b) the vertical axis line crossed the legend; in Figure 2(a) the label of the stability limit was cut at the right edge of the plot and the label of the largest step lay on the curve; and the summary table of item [1] had fixed column widths (81 character widths in all), wider than the page, so it was cut at the right edge. Fix: no axis lines inside the framed plots (only the horizontal line of zero deviation in Figure 1(b)); the labels moved into empty regions and written in the muted grey of the text; the horizontal range of Figure 2(a) extended to 4, so that the label fits to the right of its line; no legend for the single curve of Figure 2(a), which its title names; the summary table without fixed widths, so that the front end wraps the statements at the width of the page, as in the table of section I.9. Evidence after the fix: `logs/E16_figures_and_table_after_fix.png`.

**E17. Formulas in text cells lost the alphabets of their letters.** The Wolfram TeX importer, which the builder used for formulas in text cells at that time (error E4), turns `\mathbf b` into `StyleBox["b", FontSlant -> "Bold"]`, an invalid option value that the front end ignores, so that the letter is printed plain; it turns `\mathsf N` and `\mathbb R` into plain letters, `\mathcal L` and `\mathfrak g` into italic letters, and `\varepsilon` into the lunate epsilon of `\epsilon`. In the texts of Part II the bold bi-quaternions $\mathbf b$, $\mathbf e_p$, $\mathbf f_p$ were therefore printed as plain letters and $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$ with a plain R (`logs/E17_text_formulas_before_and_after_fix.png`), and the energy $\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$ was printed with the character of the Levi-Civita symbol $\epsilon^{\rm LL}_{jj'j''}$; the texts of Part I were affected in the same way. Evidence: `tools/e17_tex_importer.wls` with its output `logs/E17_tex_importer.txt`. Fix (in the builder): before the import every styled letter is replaced by a placeholder word inside `\mathrm{...}` (for example `\mathbb R` by `\mathrm{QZbbQRZQ}`), which the importer keeps as one string, and `\varepsilon` by the placeholder of the curly epsilon; after the import each placeholder is replaced by the box that the converter of the notebook uses for the same letter (section 6): double-struck, script and Gothic characters, bold and sans-serif upright letters, and the curly epsilon. In the built notebook the 588 formulas of the text cells then contain 270 bold letters, 116 sans-serif letters, 76 double-struck, script and Gothic characters, 12 curly and 8 lunate epsilons, no invalid bold option and no placeholder (`logs/E17_tex_importer.txt`). (This fix kept the importer; error E22 showed that the importer also reads some formulas wrongly, and the reader of formulas of section 6.2 replaced it.)

**E18. Components of families typed in input cells were not symbols.** In the table of the basic matrices (section II.1.3 of the notebook) the label $\gamma^0_{\rm LL}$ was printed as 1 and $\gamma^1_{\rm LL}$ as $\gamma_{\rm LL}$, and the axis label $q^1_{\rm LL}$ of Figure 1 lost its superscript (`logs/E18_components_before_fix.png`). Cause: the builder writes the box of a component into the input cell, but the component had never been symbolized, so the kernel read the box $\gamma^0_{\rm LL}$ as the power 0 of the expression $\gamma_{\rm LL}$, which is 1, and $q^1_{\rm LL}$ as the first power of $q_{\rm LL}$. No check had caught this, because these results were only displayed. Fix (section 10.4): the builder collects all components typed in the notebook, the first cell of Part II makes those that do not yet exist, and the check T2-SYM verifies all of them. An intermediate state showed the internal name of $q^1_{\rm LL}$ in the label of the graphics, because labels of graphics are typeset in `TraditionalForm`, for which the new component had no rule (the same cause as E12); the cell now gives every component its `TraditionalForm` box and a usage message, and T2-SYM also checks that the TeX of every component contains no internal name. Evidence after the fix: `logs/E18_components_after_fix.png`.

**E19. Notations of Part II that were not registered symbols.** The first version of the texts and the labels of Part II wrote some objects with the short notations of the original program instead of the registered symbols: $\Delta x$ for the lattice spacing $\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}$, $\Delta t$ for the time step $\ell^{\rm t}_{\rm LL}$, $r_W$ for $r^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}$, $U_j(s)$ for $\mathsf U^{\rm link}_{{\rm LL},j}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$, $V_0$ for $\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},0}$, $P$ for $\mathsf P_{\rm LL}$, $\xi_p$ for $\xi^{\rm LL}_p(\mathbf b)$, $S$ and $L$ for $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)$ and $\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)$, $E$ for $E^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}$, a plain $x$ for the product of an energy and the time step, and $m$, $\varepsilon$, $\varepsilon_{\rm lat}$ without the label LL. A plain $x$, for example, could be taken for the coordinates $x^p_{\rm LL}$. This contradicts the rule of the project that every object has one unique, registered symbol. Fix: all texts and all labels of Part II use the registered symbols; in the labels of graphics they are the symbolized symbols themselves, printed with upright letters, as in the cells of the notebook (option `SingleLetterItalics -> False`; the reference page of the option states that it is typically `True` in cells of `TraditionalForm`); the one abbreviation that was not registered, $z$ for the product $E^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}\ell^{\rm t}_{\rm LL}$ of an energy and the time step, was defined where it was used. The printed lines of the checks keep the plain-text wording of the original report, whose names (for example `dx`, `r_W`, `Sspin`, `Kch`) are the plain-text names of the registry; only the abbreviation x of GS-13 was not a registered name. (The check of the Runge-Kutta step, with the abbreviations $z$ and x, went with error E20; the present GS-13 uses registered names only.)

**E20. The time integration was made with a Runge-Kutta method of a fixed step, written by hand.** The first version of Part II followed the first version of the original programs, which advanced the lattice equations with the Runge-Kutta method of the fourth order, with a fixed time step chosen from the bound $B^{\rm H}_{\rm LL}$ so that the method is stable (time step times $B^{\rm H}_{\rm LL}$ at most 2.5). The library of the notebook contained the step of this method for the time integrations of the later items (function `t2rk4Step`; item [1] itself integrates nothing in time), its check GS-13 tested the modulus of the factor by which one such step multiplies an eigenvector of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$, and its Figure 2 showed this modulus. Such a method has no control of its error: the step is fixed by an argument of stability, the error of the result is not estimated, and the norm of the state, which the exact evolution conserves, decays slowly. The author rejected the method on 18 November 2026 and required the integrators of the suite SUNDIALS instead (item 6 of section 1); the original programs had been changed to CVODE in the same way. Fix in the notebook: every time integration is made by IDA through `NDSolve` (section 11.5); `t2rk4Step` was removed; the check GS-13 was rewritten as the comparison of the integration with the exact solution that the original program now makes with CVODE (section 12.2); the new check T2-IDA tests the integrator and the meaning of its tolerances; the data cell and the check T2-DATA-1 use the file of the integrations by CVODE instead of the file of the Runge-Kutta factor; Figure 2 was redrawn; the registry received the entry 204 and restated entries 116, 119, 201, 202 and 203 (section 7). Rule for all later stages: no integrator of a fixed step written by hand, anywhere. Evidence: the checks T2-IDA and GS-13 of the final evaluation.

**E21. Errors of the first draft of the IDA version, found by review before the notebook was built.** The draft was read line by line against the original check and against measurements (`logs/IDA_lattice_probe.txt`) before it was put into the notebook. (a) It assumed that the derivative which `NDSolve` stores at a grid time equals the matrix `m` applied to the stored value. The measurement showed that it does not (Newton's method stops at a tolerance): the difference was up to $2.3\times10^{-9}$ at the 41 grid times that the experiment looked at, and it is up to $3.0\times10^{-7}$ in the check GS-13, which looks at the two ends of every internal step that contains an output time. The bound $B^{\rm out}_{\rm LL}$ received the term $\tfrac14h^{\rm IDA}_{\rm LL}r^{\rm der}_{\rm LL}$, and `t2evolveIDA` returns the differences (`"residualAt"`). (b) It used $N^{\rm st}_{\rm LL}B^{\rm loc}_{\rm LL}$ as a bound of the deviation without knowing the local errors; IDA controls only its estimate of them. The function `t2localErrorsIDA` now measures the exact local error of every internal step, and the bound follows from the unitarity of the evolution. (c) It had left out one requirement of the original check, the change of the norm below $10^{-10}$ for $r^{\rm tol}_{\rm LL}=10^{-12}$; restored. (d) Its text said that IDA solves the linear equations of Newton's method with a dense direct solver; this had not been verified, and the text now states only what ``Options[NDSolve`IDA]`` shows (`logs/IDA_tolerance_scaling.txt`). (e) A local variable of the new function had the name of a variable of Part I (`xs`, the list of the coordinate components of test S-8); a local variable does not change the other one, but the rule of the notebook is one name for one object, so the variables are `tIDA` and `yIDA`. (f) The weights of the two stored derivatives were bounded by the sum of their two largest values, $8/27$ of the step; the sum of their moduli is at most $1/4$ of the step (section 11.5), which gives the sharper bound. (g) Its text said that IDA computes with real numbers only, without evidence. The experiment gave IDA a complex equation: `NDSolve` answers with the message `NDSolveValue::mconly` and returns no solution (`logs/IDA_lattice_probe.txt`); the text of the notebook quotes the message. (h) Two comparisons of the experiments on the meaning of the tolerances, which set one of the two goals to 15 digits or to `Infinity` in order to switch one tolerance off, decided nothing (with `PrecisionGoal -> Infinity` the integration stops with the message `NDSolveValue::ndtola` where a component of the solution is essentially zero); the direct measurement of the local errors replaced them (section 11.5).

**E22. The TeX importer showed 28 formulas wrongly.** Found on 18 November 2026 on the exported page of a new text: the formula $\big(1+\tfrac14h^{\rm IDA}_{\rm LL}E^{\rm max}_{\rm LL}\big)B^{\rm err}_{\rm LL}$ was shown as $\big(\frac{1+1}{4h^{\rm IDA}_{\rm LL}E^{\rm max}_{\rm LL}}\big)B^{\rm err}_{\rm LL}$. Cause: the TeX importer of the Wolfram Language, which the builder used for the formulas of text cells and section I.8 of the notebook for the meanings of the registry, takes for a fraction whose arguments are written without braces everything up to the end of the group as the denominator and everything before the fraction as part of the numerator (section 6.2); TeX takes the single token that follows. The comparison of all 791 different formulas of the notebook of that stage (the stage of item [1]; `logs/E22_formula_compare_report.txt`) found 27 formulas with this error and one more error, the command `\hbar` shown as nothing (section 6.2 gives the numbers of the present notebook). The same comparison for the notebook as it was committed before (741 different formulas; `logs/E22_formula_compare_report_committed_notebook.txt`) found 24 formulas that this notebook showed wrongly. Two stood in texts of Part II: in the link matrix (section 11.3) the argument of the cosine and of the sine was shown as $1/(2\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\lvert\vec A^{\rm v}_{{\rm LL},j}\rvert)$ instead of one half times $\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\lvert\vec A^{\rm v}_{{\rm LL},j}\rvert$, and in the text of GS-3 the element of the Lorentz matrix was shown as the fraction with the numerator $[\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)]_{pp'}=1$ and the denominator $2\,{\rm Re}\,{\rm tr}(\dots)$ instead of $[\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)]_{pp'}=\tfrac12{\rm Re}\,{\rm tr}(\dots)$. The other 22 stood in the meanings of the table of the symbols (section I.8 of the notebook), for example $Q_{\rm LL}=-\tfrac12$ shown as the fraction with the numerator $Q_{\rm LL}=-1$ and the denominator 2, $d^\iota_{\rm LL}=-\tfrac12G_{\rm LL}\langle\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}\rangle$ with $G_{\rm LL}\langle\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}\rangle$ in the denominator, and the units "in which $\hbar$ and the speed of light are 1" without the $\hbar$. The four other formulas of the 28 are new formulas of 18 November 2026 (three with the bound $B^{\rm out}_{\rm LL}$ and its parts in the texts, one in the meaning of the registry entry 204); the first of them made the error visible. The numbers of the checks were not affected, because the code does not use the importer; what was wrong was what the reader was shown. No check had caught it, because the formulas of the texts were not tested at all. Fix: the notebook's own reader of formulas (section 6.2) replaces the importer in the builder and in section I.8; test S-15 and the warnings of the builder test it at every build and every evaluation; the comparison with the importer is kept as a tool. Evidence: `logs/E22_formulas_importer_and_own_reader.png` (one text converted by both programs), `logs/E22_notebook_texts_before_and_after_fix.png` (the two texts of the notebook before and after).

**E23. The table of the symbols (section I.8 of the notebook) showed letters in wrong alphabets and broke its lines badly.** Section I.8 converted the meanings with the TeX importer alone, without the protection of the alphabets that the builder had (error E17): bold, sans-serif, double-struck, script and Gothic letters were shown as plain letters, and `\varepsilon` with the character of `\epsilon`. The meanings of the committed notebook contain 826 formulas, 598 of them different; in the reading of section 6.2 the importer alone gives 278 of the 598 a reading that differs from what their TeX says (351 of the 826 places): the 22 wrong formulas of error E22, and 256 formulas with a letter in a wrong alphabet or the wrong epsilon (counted from the report `logs/E22_formula_compare_report_committed_notebook.txt`). In an output cell the front end sets a single letter in italics automatically, so the upright E of the gamma function $\Gamma_{\rm E}$ was shown in italics. Code was shown with its backquotes. The meaning was a row of pieces of text and formulas, and the lines were broken at the boundaries of the pieces. Fix: a meaning is now one inline text cell made by `SL31TextBoxes` (section 6.2), with the formulas of the reader of formulas (every letter with an explicit style), code in the code typeface, and the lines broken by the front end. Evidence: `logs/E23_table_of_symbols_before_and_after_fix.png`.

**E24. The comma after a long formula stood at the end of the first line of the formula.** A formula inside a text is an inline cell. Where such a formula was too long for one line, it was shown on several lines, and the text that follows it, a comma, was shown at the right end of its first line. In the notebook as committed before this happened to the formula of the link matrix and to the formula of the particle row of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ (both of section 11.3; the picture shows them). Fix: a formula that needs more than one line is written in the source as a cell of the style `DisplayFormula`, with its comma inside the formula: the particle row, and the new formula of the bound $B^{\rm out}_{\rm LL}$, which stood alone in a text cell. The formula of the link matrix now fits into one line, because the formulas are set in Times and no longer in the wider typeface of code. The builder converts a display formula with the reader of formulas and sets it in Times at 1.25 times the size of the style. In the style sheet of the notebook (`Default.nb`) the style `DisplayFormula` has 14 points and the text has 15 points, so that a display formula has 17.5 points and a formula inside a text, which is set at 1.1 times the size of its text, 16.5 points (`tools/style_font_sizes.wls`, output `logs/E24_style_font_sizes.txt`); at the size of the style alone a display formula would be smaller than the formulas of the text around it. Evidence: `logs/E22_notebook_texts_before_and_after_fix.png`.

**E25. Labels of Figure 2 were ambiguous, and one lay on a line.** Found on the exported page of the first evaluation with IDA: in panel (a) the three labels of the tolerances stood between the six curves, so that a reader could not tell to which pair of curves a label belonged; in panel (b) the label of CVODE for $r^{\rm tol}_{\rm LL}=10^{-10}$ lay on the dashed line that arrives at its point. Fix: in panel (a) the time axis is drawn beyond the last time, and each pair of curves is joined by two thin grey lines to its label at the right; in panel (b) the label stands below and to the left of its point. Evidence: `logs/E25_figure2_before_and_after_fix.png`.

**E26. The letters of a group name were set apart like the factors of a product.** In a formula such as $SL(2,\mathbb C)$ each letter was a box of its own, and the front end puts the space of a product between neighbouring boxes; TeX sets neighbouring letters without a space. Fix: the reader of formulas joins neighbouring letters of one alphabet into one string (section 6.2).

**E27. A copy to the author's computer delivered an earlier version of a file once more.** It happened on 18 November 2026 during the experiments with IDA, although the cause was known since the first stage of Part II (Appendix A): the comparison of the digests on both sides finds such a copy, but it does not prevent it. Fix: the staging files of the copies are named by the digest of their content, so that a staged name is never used for two contents (Appendix A); the comparison of the digests is kept. No such copy was seen afterwards.

**E28. The coordinates x5, x6, x7 were shown like products.** Found on 18 November 2026 on the picture of the table of the symbols that was made as evidence for error E23: the meaning of the number of space dimensions $D$ names the coordinates $x5,x6,x7$ of the eight-dimensional spacetime of the main project, and the notebook showed them as $x\,5$, $x\,6$, $x\,7$, each with the space of a product between the letter and the number (the notebook as committed before showed them in the same way). Cause: the reader of formulas gave a row of two boxes, the letter and the number, and the front end puts the thin space of a product between two neighbouring items of a row (section 2.1); TeX sets them without a space. A test of several ways of writing the pair (`tools/letter_digit_spacing_test.wls`, pages `logs/E28_letter_digit_spacing_test.pdf`) showed that the option `AutoSpacing -> False` of a style box does not remove the space, and that the character `\[InvisibleSpace]` between the two items does. Fix: the reader of formulas joins a number that directly follows a letter to this letter with `\[InvisibleSpace]` (section 6.2); a number before a letter stays a factor. The rule applies to one of the formulas of the notebook (791 different formulas at that stage). Part (c) of test S-15 checks the two box structures, section I.3 of the notebook states the rule, and the readings of all formulas were unchanged (the comparison of section 6.2 was repeated at that stage: the same 763 equal and 28 different readings). Evidence: `logs/E28_letter_and_number_before_and_after_fix.png` (the test, and the row of the table before and after).

**E29. Three rows of the table of the files were printed as one (an error of this document).** Found on 18 November 2026 on the printed page of the table of the files (now Appendix D): the row of the file `sl31_core.wl` also contained the texts of the two rows after it, set in the typeface of code, in the version of this document as committed before and again in the first print of the present version. Cause: the content of that row named the package with its context mark, written with a single backquote. In Markdown a single backquote opens a piece of code, which for the converter pandoc runs on to the next backquote, here across the ends of the rows. The same mistake in a text cell of the notebook had been error E14; it was repeated in this document. No check saw it: the check of the layout looks for lines that are too wide and for missing characters, and a merged row is neither. Fix: the two rows write the name of the package between double backquotes. The new script `tools/check_md_source.py` reports every line of this document with an odd number of single backquotes, and it compares the number of rows of every table of this document with the number of rows of the same table in the LaTeX file; the script that builds the `.tex` and `.pdf` versions runs both checks and stops if one of them reports something. The present version has 12 tables, each with the same rows in both files.

**E30. Formulas of the text cells used letters that are not registered symbols.** Found on 18 November 2026 by the new script `tools/check_notebook_symbols.py` (Appendix A), which was written for the texts of chapter II.3 and then run on the whole notebook. In the notebook as it was committed before (the stage of item [1]), 12 formulas of text cells broke a rule of the symbols (`logs/E30_symbol_check_committed_notebook.txt`): the sum of two subscripted letters b in section I.2 (the letter is the name of a variable of the code of that section, not a symbol); an abbreviation, the letter E with the subscript $\Lambda$, for a square root in the text of test S-6 (two formulas); a Kronecker delta without the suffix in the text of test S-7; the letter M alone as an abbreviation of $M^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}$ in the text of test S-10 (two formulas); the real and the imaginary part of a complex number written as two letters in section II.1.4 (two formulas); the letter n alone in section II.1.5; the letter k alone in the text of GS-12; and the letter $\kappa$ in the text of GS-16 (two formulas). No number of the notebook was wrong, but a reader cannot know what a letter without a registered meaning denotes, and Hard Rule 5 of the project forbids it. Cause: the rules of the symbols were checked by a script only in the documents of the folder `Revision_SuperLoop`; the texts of the notebook were checked by reading. Fix: every such formula was rewritten, with the registered symbol (the mass $M^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}$ written out, the square root written out), in words (the real and the imaginary part of a coefficient; every eigenvector), or as code (the sum of section I.2 is written as the expression `Subscript[b, 1, p] + 2 Subscript[b, 2, p]` that the cell returns); the integer of the allowed momenta of GS-16 is counted in words. The script is run before every build; for the final sources it reports 917 formulas and none that breaks a rule.

**E31. The last line of a printed check passed the right edge of the page and was cut.** Found on 18 November 2026 on the exported pages of the first evaluation of chapter II.3 (page 228): the details of check AS-10 ended with "84 discarded states", and the rest of the line was lost. Cause: the function `SL31Check` printed the details as a row that contained further rows and typeset numbers. The front end indents every continuation line of a nested row a little more, and it does not count this indentation when it decides where to break the line; a long line of a deeply nested row therefore ends beyond the right edge, where the printed page cuts it. This was found with two small test notebooks that printed the same details in several ways. A text cell of the style of printed lines does not break at all. Fix: `SL31Check` writes every number of the details as text (an integer in full; a number given with a number of significant digits in the notation `1.5e-14`; a number given with a number of decimals in that form), joins neighbouring texts into one text, and prints one flat row with the option `LineIndent -> 0`, so that the front end breaks the line only at spaces and does not indent it. A typeset expression inside the details stays typeset. The formats were tested on 47 cases by a script before the notebook was built. A mechanical check of the exported pages was added (`tools/check_pdf_right_edge.py`, Appendix A): the first exported pages of the chapter fail it on four pages (`logs/E31_right_edge_first_export.txt`), the present pages on none. Evidence: `logs/E31_check_line_before_and_after_fix.png`. The lines of all 48 checks were printed again in the new form; their numbers are those of the evaluation.

**E32. The Figures 3, 4 and 5 were wider than the printed page.** Found on the same exported pages (pages 234, 237 and 240): each of these figures placed two panels side by side in a grid 880 to 1000 points wide, where the text area of a page is 494 points wide, and the page showed the first panel and a part of the second; a legend and the end of a caption were cut. Figure 2 of item [1], which also has two panels, had been printed correctly because its two panels stand in a row that the front end may break. Fix: the panels of a figure stand one below the other, each 480 points wide with its legend below it (functions `asPanel`, `asSize` and `asFigure` of chapter II.3); the Figures 4 and 5, with three and four panels, take two cells each, so that no cell is higher than a page. The mechanical check of error E31 covers figures as well. Evidence: `logs/E32_figure3_before_and_after_fix.png`.

**E33. Names of the program were used for more than one object.** Found on 18 November 2026 by the new script `tools/check_notebook_names.py` (Appendix A), written when chapter II.3 was added: a name that receives a value at the top level of a cell keeps this value in all later cells, so that a later chapter that uses the name for another object, or that forgets to define a variable of its own, computes with the old value without any message. In the notebook as it was committed before, the script reports 12 violations in three chapters (`logs/E33_names_check_committed_notebook.txt`): the names `ratio`, `eps` and `p2`, which are global in Part I, were used again in Part II, and the global names `gm`, `k`, `k3`, `lat1`, `n` and `ph` of chapter II.2 were also used as local names (variables of loops or arguments of functions) in the same chapter. None of them changed a result: in each case the later use set its own value before it read the name, and a local variable hides a global one. But the rule "one name, one object" of the decisions was stated without a check, and it was broken. Fix: the names were made unique (for example the local names of the chapters II.1 and II.2 were renamed), every chapter of Part II begins its own names with a prefix of its own (`as` in chapter II.3), and the script is run before every build: 4 chapters, 682 global names, 0 errors. The renaming changed no number: 35 of the 37 checks of the earlier chapters print the same statement and the same numbers, digit for digit, in the evaluation logs before and after it (the computing time aside); the two others were changed on purpose (T2-SYM now counts 17 components, and T2-IDA has a third part, section 11.5).

**E34. The first formulation of the checks AS-2 and AS-4 required of IDA what its error test does not promise.** The two checks integrate the lattice equations in time. The first formulation followed check GS-13: it required that the exact local error of every internal step is at most $B^{\rm loc}_{\rm LL}$ and derived the bound of the deviation from this. Before the notebook was built, the exact local errors of both integrations were measured in the weighted root-mean-square norm that IDA itself uses (`tools/item2_ida_local_errors.wl`, `logs/IDA_item2_local_errors.txt`). In AS-2 the largest value is 1.05: an exact local error exceeded the tolerance in the norm of IDA, although the step was accepted. This is no fault of IDA, which tests its own estimate of the error; it shows that the requirement is not implied by the error test. In these two states one half of the real unknowns, 256 of 512, is exactly zero at all times (in AS-2 the two hole groups, in AS-4 the particle components with isospin down and the hole components with isospin up); the tolerance of such an unknown is $a^{\rm tol}_{\rm LL}$ alone, $10^{-14}$, which a rounding error reaches. The ratio of the Euclidean norm of a local error to $B^{\rm loc}_{\rm LL}$ was at most 0.11 in AS-2 but 0.96 in AS-4, so that the first formulation would have held by a margin of 4 per cent only, by chance. Fix: both checks use the bound that needs no condition, the sum of the measured exact local errors of all internal steps, carried to the output times as in section 11.5 (the function `t2evolveIDAChecked`, section 13.4); the largest ratio is printed for information. Check GS-13 keeps its requirement, which holds there with a margin (largest ratios 0.83, 0.32, 0.32) and none of whose unknowns is zero.

**E35. Errors of the first draft of the texts of chapter II.3, found on its exported pages.** (a) Three displays held two equations side by side and were broken by the front end in the middle of an equation; each equation now stands in a display of its own. (b) The long gap equation in closed form was broken badly; it is written as a sum over the two signs. (c) The proof of the bound states of a domain wall used formulas too long for a line; it is written with short formulas. (d) The text of check AS-10 said that the highest twenty levels are "the levels 1 to 119" where the levels 0 to 119 exist. (e) The details of check AS-8 printed "1 bound states". All were found by reading the 247 exported pages of the first evaluation and are corrected in the sources.

**E36. Errors of the original program t2_analytic, found while it was rewritten.** A program that is rewritten statement by statement is read more closely than a program that is only run. Six errors of the original report and of its document were found in this way and corrected in the original (repository commit a7ae522; errors E66 to E71 of the provenance of the folder `Revision_SuperLoop`): the statement of AS-5 said "12 couplings" where 11 of the 12 cases have a gap, and the number was not counted by the program; the statement of AS-10 called all 84 discarded states artefacts, although 80 of them are exact levels 110 to 119 that the criterion of the check does not ask for and only 4 are artefacts of the truncated basis; the bounds of AS-9 were stated for one eigenvalue and applied to a group of eight nearly equal eigenvalues without a justification (the two theorems are now stated and proved for a group, and the spread of the group is measured); the details of AS-8 had the same error of grammar as (e) of E35; two comments of the script of the figures named wrong checks; and the script that runs all checks of that folder reported a failure of its first pass where none was meant. The notebook uses the corrected statements, and its checks AS-5, AS-9 and AS-10 count what they state.

**E37. The front end stopped responding when an evaluation was started, and the author was asked for more than was needed.** On 18 November 2026 at 12:19 Pacific time the evaluation of the notebook was started as before, by one call that opened the notebook, started the kernel `SL31Run` and queued the evaluations of two notebooks at once. The front end stopped responding (the system showed the application as not responding, with no processor use), and with it the kernel in which the watcher of the requests runs. What is known: the kernel `SL31Run` had used about 2 seconds of processor time in all, which is the time of its start, so it never received an input line; a sample of its threads, taken later, shows its main thread waiting at a small depth of calls, not inside an evaluation. The front end therefore stopped while it started the kernel. The exact cause inside the front end was not found, because the front end was ended before it could be examined. The same call had worked in about ten earlier runs of that day, which points to a race between the start of the kernel and the two queued evaluations. Fix: the start of the kernel and the queueing of the notebook are separate calls, the second made only after the kernel has written a file that shows that it runs and answers; the kernel records its progress in a file after every input line; and the progress is read from that file, without a call to the front end while the front end is busy (Appendix A). The next run, at 13:17, evaluated the notebook completely. A second error was made in handling this: the author was asked to end the whole application by force and to open it again. Only one process had to be ended, the kernel `SL31Run` of this run; ending the application also ended the front end with its open notebooks and all other kernels, and it left the kernel `SL31Run` running without a parent. The request should have named that one process. The application was then reopened and the watcher restarted without a further request to the author.

**E38. The table of check GS-13 gives numbers that it does not explain (open).** The table of section 12.2, and a message to the author that quoted three of its rows, compare the deviations of IDA and of CVODE for three tolerances. The message used its three headings without defining them, and neither the message nor section 12.2 explains why the deviations are a hundred to a thousand times larger than the tolerances, why they do not fall by the same factor from one tolerance to the next, or why IDA is less accurate than CVODE for the first tolerance and more accurate for the other two; the sentence "what the numbers say" of section 12.2 only restates the numbers. The definitions were sent to the author on the same day. The explanation needs the internal steps of both integrators (their sizes and orders, the exact local error of every step, and how these add up); the program that measures them for CVODE and for IDA is being written (in Rust, with the integrators of SUNDIALS 7.8.0 of the repository), and section 12.2, the text of the notebook and the document of Test 2 will be completed with its results in the next stage. Until then the table stands as measured, and this entry stays open.

**E39. Symbols were used in this document without a definition in section 2 (an error of this document).** Found on 18 November 2026 by the new script `tools/check_provenance_symbols.py` (Appendix A), written because the new section 13 uses twenty symbols that the earlier sections did not use. Run on the document as it was committed before (`logs/E39_provenance_symbols_committed_document.txt`), the script reports three symbols that a formula used and section 2 did not define: the wave vector $\vec k^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}$ in the meaning of a registry entry that section 7 quotes, the parts $\psi^{\rm p}_{{\rm LL},n}$, $\psi^{\rm h}_{{\rm LL},n}$ of a numbered mode in the sections 11.3 and 12.2 (section 2.5 defined the letter $k$ of a component as the only index of these symbols), and the transpose of the field written with its mark before the suffix, which reads as another symbol. The first version of the script reported eleven; eight of them were ways of writing that the script did not yet read as it should (a list of three or four index values, an argument in the subscript of the right multiplication $R^{\rm LL}_{\mathbf x}$, the exponential of a phase), and one more is a wrongly printed label that the description of error E18 quotes on purpose, which the script now lists as an exception with its reason. Fix: the two missing definitions were added to the sections 2.2 and 2.5, the transposition is written after the suffix, and the twenty symbols of section 13 are defined in the new section 2.8. The script is run whenever the document is built; for the present version it reports 149 used symbols, all of them defined.

**E40. The evaluation time of the earlier versions was an upper bound, not the time of the evaluation (an error of this document).** The earlier versions of this document gave "evaluation time 125 seconds". That number was the time from the start of a run to the moment at which the finished run was noticed by the next query, which came up to a minute after the end. Since the kernel writes the time after every input line (E37), the time of the evaluation itself is known: 37 seconds for the 886 input lines of the present notebook.

**E41. The first version of the experiment on the settled steps read a wrong number off its own output (found before anything was committed).** The script `tools/gs13_ida_settled_steps.wl` (section 11.5) first printed, for the settled steps whose end times lie between two powers of 2, the smallest and the largest rounding error of an addition. All steps of such a range but one have the same rounding error; the one step that begins below the power of 2 has another. The smallest value was taken for the value of the range, and for $r^{\rm tol}_{\rm LL}=10^{-12}$ the values $-0.27$, $+0.11$ and $+0.06$ units in the last place were noted for the three ranges. Found by: the sum of the rounding errors of a range, printed beside them, was not the number of steps times the noted value (from 16 to 20: 8216 steps and the sum $-1.29\times10^{-11}$, which is $-0.443$ units per step, not $+0.06$). Fix: the script prints the median over the steps of a range, which is the value of all steps but one, and the number of steps with another value (0 or 1); the values are $+0.2275$, $+0.1138$ and $-0.4431$. The wrong values were in no file of the repository.

# Appendix C. Decisions

- "./" of the task is the top folder of the repository, so the notebook is `3+1super-loop.nb` there; "./test" is the author's folder of the Test 2 documents, whose symbols are the complete registry (section 7). The author's folders `test` and `mathematica_symbols` are private and are not part of the repository.
- Function symbols are symbolized by their heads: $W_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)$ is the head $W_{\rm LL}$ applied to $\mathbf u$, written `W_LL[u]` in the Wolfram Language.
- Indices ($p$, $j$, $\iota$) are not symbolized: they are the running letters inside the scripts; their components are symbolized on demand (`SL31Instance`). Primed indices are symbolized, because they also appear alone.
- Operators, number sets and the imaginary unit are not symbolized: they are Wolfram functions, domains and the symbol `I` (S-13).
- The single letter $D$ is symbolized in the box `TagBox["D", "SL31Plain"]`, so that the letter `D`, typed plainly, keeps its meaning (the derivative) in the Wolfram Language.
- Values are given to symbols only inside `Block`, so that after every test the symbols are free again.
- The registry is contained in the notebook as data (one input cell of about 140 000 characters) and also shown as formatted tables, group by group (section I.8).
- Part II rewrites the original programs in the order of their code and draws the random numbers in the same order with the same seeds, so that every random object of a check equals the one of the original program; results that are pure rounding errors are reported next to the values of the original report (section 10.2).
- The numbers of the three data files of the figures of item [1] are copied into the notebook (about 46 000 characters in one input cell) instead of being read from the files, so that the notebook is complete and stand-alone; they are used by the check T2-DATA-1 and, for the curves of CVODE, by Figure 2. The cell fills 22 pages of the printed notebook (pages 173 to 194).
- The symbolic algebra `sym.rs` of the original library is not rewritten, because the Wolfram Language is itself a symbolic algebra (section 11.4); GS-12 uses symbols of the Wolfram Language instead.
- Every name of Part II stands for one object only. For example, the variable of the sum over the plane waves of GS-5 is called `wave`, because the name `wv` belongs to the random vector of GS-12.
- Labels of graphics are built from expressions (`Row`, `Subscript`, `Superscript` and the registered symbols), not from strings in linear syntax, so that the source stays readable and the labels are typeset in the same way in the notebook and in the PDF.
- The checks T2-SYM, T2-RNG, T2-LINALG and T2-DATA-1 are checks of the rewriting itself and are not in the original report; GS-1 to GS-16 keep the names, the statements and the tolerances of the original report.
- The components of families are collected by the builder from the source rather than listed by hand, so that a component typed in a later item of Part II is made automatically (section 10.4).
- The notebook integrates in time with IDA of SUNDIALS, through `NDSolve` with `Method -> "IDA"`. The original programs use CVODE of the same suite, which a notebook that is complete in itself cannot call; IDA is the integrator of this suite that the Wolfram Language contains. The equations are given to it in real form, because it accepts real equations only (section 11.5).
- The checks of the time integration do not trust the error estimate of the integrator: they measure the exact local error of every internal step and derive their bounds from the tolerances of the integration; they have no tolerance of their own that could be adjusted (sections 11.5 and 12.2).
- The states at the output times are taken from the `InterpolatingFunction` of `NDSolve`, as a user of the notebook would take them; the check therefore includes the error of the interpolation between the internal steps (the bound $B^{\rm out}_{\rm LL}$), and it verifies that the interpolation is the cubic Hermite polynomial that the bound assumes.
- The file of the original program with the modulus of the factor of a Runge-Kutta step, its part of the data cell, its check and its figure were removed with the method itself (error E20); nothing of the removed method is kept in the notebook.
- Every formula that the notebook shows is converted by its own reader of formulas (section 6.2). A formula is set in the typeface Times with an explicit style for every letter, so that the alphabets of the project (italic, upright, bold, sans-serif, double-struck, script, Gothic) differ visibly in every kind of cell.
- The meanings of the table of the symbols are inline text cells (error E23), and a formula that stands alone is a display formula (error E24).
- Between the factors of a product the notebook keeps the thin space that the front end of the Wolfram Language puts there, although TeX sets a product without it: a notebook should show a product as the Wolfram Language shows it. Inside a name there is no space: letters of one alphabet form one string, and a number that directly follows a letter is joined to it (errors E26 and E28).
- The pictures that show a page before and after a fix are made by a script from the exported pages, and the pieces of a page are named in the script by words of the page (Appendix A), so that a reader can see what was cut from where and can make the pictures again.
- The comparison of the reader of formulas with the TeX importer is kept as a tool (`tools/formula_compare.wls`), although the importer is no longer used: two programs that share no code and agree on 1040 of 1083 formulas (763 of 791 when the decision was taken) are a test of the reader that a list of examples could not give.
- In Figure 2 the curves are labelled directly, each pair joined to its label, instead of by a legend with six entries; the two integrators keep their colors, kinds of line and markers in both panels.
- The two input cells that hold only the numbers of the data files of the original programs (3 files of item [1], 11 files of item [2]; about 46 000 and 174 000 characters) are closed cells: they are evaluated like every other cell, but the notebook shows only their brackets and the printed form leaves them out (22 pages of numbers in the printed form of the stage of item [1]). The notebook stays complete and stand-alone, and its printed form stays readable; the text before each of the two cells says how to open it.
- A printed check line writes its numbers as text, a small number in the notation `1.5e-14` (error E31). The same notation is used by the original reports, so that the numbers of the notebook and those of the original stand in one form in the same line. A typeset expression in the details stays typeset.
- The panels of a figure stand one below the other, each with its own legend, and a figure with more than two panels takes two cells (error E32): a figure must fit the printed page, and a reader who prints the notebook must lose nothing.
- Every chapter of Part II begins its own names with a prefix of its own (`as` in chapter II.3; functions that belong to the library begin with `t2`), and two scripts check before every build that every formula of the texts uses registered symbols only and that every name of the program stands for one object (errors E30 and E33). A rule that is only stated is broken sooner or later; a rule that a script checks is not.
- The checks of item [2] with a time integration (AS-2 and AS-4) pass on a bound that needs no condition, the sum of the measured exact local errors of all internal steps carried to the output times (error E34, section 13.4), and keep the requirement of the original program (a relative deviation below $10^{-9}$). The requirement that every local error stays below $B^{\rm loc}_{\rm LL}$ is kept only where it holds with a margin and no unknown is zero (GS-13).
- The texts of a chapter are printed in this document as the notebook has them, word for word, by a script (section 13.2), from the stage of item [2] on: the reader of this document then reads the same physics, the same proofs and the same statements of the checks as the reader of the notebook, and the two cannot differ. The sections 11 and 12, written before this decision, describe their chapters in words of their own.
- The sections that concern the notebook as a whole are appendices (A to E) from this stage on, so that every chapter of Part II can receive a section of its own without a change of the numbers of the sections that many sentences refer to.
- The evaluation in the front end is made in three calls (start the kernel; queue the notebook; finish), and the kernel records its progress in a file (error E37): a run that stops can then be located without the screen, and nothing has to be asked of the author.
- In check T2-DATA-2 the two columns of the data files that hold results of the integration by CVODE (the probabilities of AS-2 and AS-4) are compared with the results of IDA within a bound derived from the measured deviations of both integrations from the exact solution, not to their last printed decimal: two integrators agree within their accuracies, not digit by digit. Every other column must agree within half a unit of its last printed decimal.
